A mapping is not a passing test. A section citation is not a mapping.
A source unit may contain several obligations; its scope and remaining cases matter.
Unmapped means no reviewed evidence link, not that no test exists. Candidate scenarios are citations only.
Execution receipts name their own RFC, corpus and implementation commits, backend and runtime.
`recorded_pass` describes that receipt only; it certifies no conformance level.
Unannotated keyword lines are a reading aid, not a complete list of implicit requirements.
A decided gap is a unit gaps.json lists with its class and card: no scenario cites it yet, and the listing maps nothing.

See [requirements.json](requirements.json) for exact clauses and applicability,

[evidence.json](evidence.json) for assertions, tests, review scope and remaining cases.

| RK-12.3.13-031 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open interruption (S1) and the virtual clock (X-CLK): an echo's final more than 10 seconds after playback ended is judged anew, and speech starting once nothing plays is nobody's echo. |
| RK-12.3.13-032 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open interruption (S1) and a gate holding a reply mid-way (S20): an echo's later words cut neither the response that followed the echoed one nor the next. |
| RK-12.3.13-033 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open STT options (S5) and the virtual clock (X-CLK): an STT stream that never closes an echoed utterance, past the verdict's 10 seconds. |
| RK-12.3.13-034 | §12.3.13 | writable | RMK-2042 | voice.open with vad, partials and transcripts and a playback_gate, the runners' interruption config leaving echo_guard_ms at its default and the session having played nothing yet: partials repeating the reply, then a final adding a word of its own while it plays: the reply is cut and the final reaches the room. |
| RK-12.3.13-035 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open interruption (S1): transcript_wait_ms set, speech without words during playback cuts only once the wait has passed. |
| RK-12.3.13-036 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.play (S12): a file played whole before a reply moves the session past echo_guard_ms, and the reply's own words then cut it. |
| RK-12.3.13-037 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open interruption (S1): a small echo_guard_ms the session's played audio passes, then the reply's own words cut it and are the turn. |
| RK-12.3.13-038 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open interruption (S1): echo_guard_ms 0, the reply's own words cut it at once. |
| RK-12.3.13-039 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open STT options (S5), transcripts with speaker segments: a new voice repeating the reply over it is no turn; a voice first heard alone is. |
| RK-12.3.13-055 | §12.3.13 | step | RMK-2042 | step: voice.open STT options (S5), transcripts with speaker segments: a new voice repeating the reply over it cuts it on its first words, and its final, discarded as echo, does not undo the cut. |

| RK-23.1-006 | §23.1 | step | RMK-1922 | Cancel the caller while the runner's cancel awaits completion and observe that the original completion finishes. |
| RK-23.2-007 | §23.2 | step | RMK-1922 | Construct a public result without giving its duration and read the default. Durations measured on completed delegations exercise elapsed time instead. |
| RK-23.3-018 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Have a private worker publish structured content on a served tool call and inspect the structured copy of its stored child end row. |
| RK-23.3-042 | §23.3 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: setup_realtime_delegation: observe the instruction flag on a delegation hand-back to a channel hosting a realtime model. |
| RK-23.3-044 | §23.3 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: setup_realtime_delegation: observe private system-intent injection on the delegating realtime voice session and the sole session when delegation is outside a call. |
| RK-23.3-045 | §23.3 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: an audio-video channel with a realtime model: observe the targeted audio-video session receiving the worker hand-back as an injection. |
| RK-23.3-046 | §23.3 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: conference model steps, a task delegated from a conference tool call: observe a conference with a realtime model receiving the private task result for its delegating or sole session. |
| RK-23.3-053 | §23.3 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: setup_realtime_delegation: compare a realtime task-result injection with the expected result, observing that it omits the text-agent-only turn instruction. |
| RK-23.3-055 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Delegate to a notifying channel and compare an empty successful task with an unsuccessful task that has no output. Assert which hand-backs arrive. |
| RK-23.3-085 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Hold inline and background workers at model gates, call framework close and observe cancellation and completion before the store is released. |
| RK-23.3-086 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Expose an instrumented task completion callback and shutdown grace/unwind observations, including the callback that persists after cancellation and its log. |
| RK-23.3-087 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Hold a newly announced task at the announcement/worker boundary, close the kit, then verify terminal status and no worker invocation. |
| RK-23.3-090 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Observe ordered log level and cause for recipient turn failure followed by the undelivered hand-back note. |
| RK-23.3-091 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Close a kit holding a notified task, observe the completion hook and absence of a new recipient turn; the diagnostic log additionally needs a log observer. |
| RK-23.3-092 | §23.3 | writable | RMK-1922 | Gate a recipient model already handling a hand-back, close the kit and inspect the cancelled response, waiter and absence of subsequent stored output. |
| RK-23.3-094 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | The telemetry fixture must expose delegation spans and their terminal status to compare success, worker failure and cancellation. |
| RK-23.3-095 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Install a runner that rejects submission or raises outside the turn after announcement and observe exactly one failing task end. |
| RK-23.3-096 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Expose a rejected submission and its callbacks, waiters and delegation span alongside the child metadata, hooks and status bus. |
| RK-23.3-097 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Provide a runner refusal and a background run raising outside its turn; separately observe the caller error and notification. |
| RK-23.3-102 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Arrange a pre-creation failure and an existing child with a different task_id, observing both that no new child was made and that the unrelated child was not written. |
| RK-23.3-103 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Gate the child store write separately from its post-create hooks and cancel preparation to observe cleanup ordering. |
| RK-23.3-104 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Instrument terminal cleanup while issuing repeated caller cancellation and framework close under the configured budgets. |
| RK-23.3-105 | §23.3 | step | RMK-1922 | Inject a store error specifically during preparation cleanup and compare the original error or cancellation, absence of announcement and logged cleanup diagnostic. |
| RK-23.4-009 | §23.4 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: setup_realtime_delegation: set up delegation on a realtime voice channel and inspect its session's declared tool and execution. |
| RK-23.4-030 | §23.4 | step | RMK-1922 | Allow deterministic advancement or injection of a CompletedTaskCache clock and supply running durations around the reference expiry and maximum-run boundaries. |
| RK-23.4-033 | §23.4 | step | RMK-1922 | Expose host insertion into the public task cache to compare caller-specific and host values and the most recent of both. |
| RK-23.4-035 | §23.4 | writable | RMK-1922 | Gate concurrent calls on one configured handler and observe at most one active delegation in the same room, with another room still able to progress. |
| RK-23.4-059 | §23.4 | writable | RMK-1922 | Keep a stale running bus entry, exceed the global ended-task retention using other rooms and then query the original task. |
| RK-23.4-066 | §23.4 | phase6 | RMK-1922 | step: realtime.read of the session configuration: demonstrate that a realtime session with otherwise visible room tasks receives no task-notes block. |
| RK-23.4-068 | §23.4 | step | RMK-1922 | Invoke the delegation tool itself without any current tool call and observe refusal. The delegate.handle step supplies a room explicitly and tests a different public API. |
| RK-24.1-004 | §24.1 | writable | RMK-1939 | The served registry fixture accepts raw content, but no shared case checks the 64-level boundary. Local input is generated from structured fields. |
| RK-24.1-005 | §24.1 | writable | RMK-1939 | No served frontmatter case checks the open-delimiter limit or its quoted-character counting. Local input is generated. |
| RK-24.1-006 | §24.1 | writable | RMK-1939 | No served-content case checks the frontmatter character limit and its exact boundary. The local fixture supplies generated frontmatter. |
| RK-24.1-007 | §24.1 | writable | RMK-1939 | Raw served content is available, but no shared case exceeds a numeric parser bound and asserts verification refusal. Local source and failure capture require fixture support. |
| RK-24.1-009 | §24.1 | step | RMK-1939 | The local fixture cannot supply arbitrary field indentation to the line-oriented reader. |
| RK-24.1-012 | §24.1 | step | RMK-1939 | The local fixture enforces its own declaration shapes and cannot exercise malformed raw source across both reader paths. |
| RK-24.2-009 | §24.2 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry snapshot can inspect a served entry's optional license; no case asserts the value or its absent form. |
| RK-24.2-003 | §24.2 | writable | RMK-1939 | A served entry and its manifest can be inspected with skill_registry_session. No case checks compatibility and license rendering. |
| RK-24.2-008 | §24.2 | writable | RMK-1939 | A registry snapshot returns metadata for a served entry. The cases do not assert retained extra values or comma joining. |
| RK-24.3-002 | §24.3 | step | RMK-1939 | Local discover runs inside setup; its directories and returned count are not shared operations. |
| RK-24.3-003 | §24.3 | step | RMK-1939 | The registry session has no public local single-directory register operation. |
| RK-24.3-004 | §24.3 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared registry session has no get_metadata operation for a single present or absent name. |
| RK-24.3-011 | §24.3 | step | RMK-1939 | A snapshot exposes the unavailable map but the shared registry session offers no direct get_unavailable_reason accessor. |
| RK-24.3-015 | §24.3 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared registry snapshot offers gated_tool_names but no closed_tool_names(activated) result. The text gate cases exercise the behavioral rules about overlapping patterns separately. |
| RK-24.3-029 | §24.3 | phase6 | RMK-1939 | step: a realtime fixture skills option: no shared realtime session exercises activation prerequisites. |
| RK-24.4-012 | §24.4 | writable | RMK-1939 | Existing cases observe bounded eviction, but none reactivates the evicted local skill to assert redelivery of its full body. |
| RK-24.4-013 | §24.4 | writable | RMK-1939 | The shared runner can replace an agent and control room history, but no local skill case demonstrates a lost record followed by a fresh body without recoverable stored activation. |
| RK-24.4-021 | §24.4 | writable | RMK-1939 | The model capture supports the text reveal window; no shared missing-skill case asserts its replacement, excluded declarations and session lifetime. |
| RK-24.4-022 | §24.4 | writable | RMK-1939 | Existing blocked-activation cases target a real skill. The unknown-skill hint after an ON_TOOL_CALL block remains unasserted. |
| RK-24.4-023 | §24.4 | phase6 | RMK-1939 | step: a realtime fixture skills option: no shared realtime case asserts the missing-skill response and reveal. |
| RK-24.4-024 | §24.4 | phase6 | RMK-1939 | step: a realtime fixture skills option: the fixed-declaration realtime gateway's hint directing the model to call_tool lacks a shared oracle. |
| RK-24.5-002 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | Shared local setup cannot observe invalid source as a discovery or registration failure. |
| RK-24.5-004 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | The local directory discovery operation, failure order and registry state cannot be observed through current steps. |
| RK-24.5-005 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | Shared local setup cannot capture a failing discovery result identifying the skill and its cause. |
| RK-24.5-006 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | The local fixture always supplies a generated directory and cannot observe discovery of an unscannable one. |
| RK-24.5-008 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | No observed local discovery operation can select and compare the explicit lenient and default modes. |
| RK-24.5-009 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | No common discovery operation exposes log records for each skipped skill. |
| RK-24.5-010 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared fixture does not observe the local registry before and after a failing discovery. |
| RK-24.5-011 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | The served registry snapshot renders the manifest. No scenario checks the exact final Unicode limit, including escaping, reasons, wrappers and origins. |
| RK-24.5-012 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | The served registry session supports listing, fetch, unavailable marks and copies. No case checks prospective manifest errors and rollback; local registration and discovery operations also need fixture exposure. |
| RK-24.5-013 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session can copy with or without marks and inspect the source after failure. No shared case observes a failed copy. |
| RK-24.5-014 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session supports served replacement and marks, but no case observes replacement near the manifest bound. Local replace is not a shared operation. |
| RK-24.5-015 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | A shared server registry can exercise refusal on a listing or fetch and inspect both catalogues. Existing cases do not assert a size-limit refusal. |
| RK-24.5-016 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | A registry and its copies can be inspected after a shared refusal that exceeds the local bound. No case asserts that transaction. |
| RK-24.5-017 | §24.5 | writable | RMK-1939 | The shared registry supports snapshots, copies and reference verification. No case verifies shared state after local manifest pressure. |
| RK-24.5-018 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared fixture exposes serialized metadata, never a caller alias to a live mutable metadata object. |
| RK-24.5-020 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | A live external metadata alias and intermediate oversized repair state are not exposed. |
| RK-24.5-021 | §24.5 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared fixture cannot construct an oversized live source via a caller alias to examine repair by replacement or subset. |
| RK-24.6-003 | §24.6 | step | RMK-1939 | The local fixture supplies ordinary files and no linked directory entries for observing resolved containment. |
| RK-24.6-004 | §24.6 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared filesystem fixture cannot observe regular-file and checked-path identity at read time. |
| RK-24.6-005 | §24.6 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared fixture does not expose whether the platform can establish the open file's location or its refusal when unavailable. |
| RK-24.6-006 | §24.6 | step | RMK-1939 | The shared fixture cannot distinguish the checked open file from a later lookup by name. |
| RK-24.6-007 | §24.6 | step | RMK-1939 | Local setup generates an ordinary SKILL.md and does not expose the file identity or failure at discovery and load. |
| RK-24.6-008 | §24.6 | writable | RMK-1939 | Text tools accept a reference or script name, but no shared case observes normalization before validation. |
| RK-24.6-009 | §24.6 | writable | RMK-1939 | Existing text-tool calls can observe refusal of an absolute name; no shared case checks platform-recognized forms. |
| RK-24.7-015 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry fixture supports multiple providers and labels; none of its scenarios attempts reassignment of a label between providers. |
| RK-24.7-016 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The scripted server supports extension declarations but no shared case asserts error for a missing Skills extension. |
| RK-24.7-019 | §24.7 | step | RMK-1939 | ScriptedSkillServer does not expose a negotiated protocol revision, so accepting the older metadata-less response is not distinguished from ordinary listing. |
| RK-24.7-020 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session can relist a changed server and inspect the prior entry across copies. The existing relisting scenario retains its entry. |
| RK-24.7-024 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session can mark an entry unavailable, copy a subset and count server requests, but no scenario compares this exclusion. |
| RK-24.7-026 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The scripted listing supports individual entries and observable refusal reasons; no case asserts a frontmatter-name and URI directory mismatch. |
| RK-24.7-027 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared listing observes an entry rejected for omitting its own SKILL.md. |
| RK-24.7-028 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared served listing asserts refusal of a resource outside its declared directory. |
| RK-24.7-081 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared listing asserts duplicate resource names within one entry; duplicate entries across cursor pages exercise another rule. |
| RK-24.7-029 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared listing case asserts the resource-count limit for a single entry. |
| RK-24.7-082 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared listing case asserts the entry's aggregate declared byte limit. |
| RK-24.7-032 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry fixture supports dynamic registration and resource reads but no shared case asserts acceptance of an allowed dynamic resource or its cumulative read limit. |
| RK-24.7-034 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session can check collisions between served origins, while admitting a local name to that same session needs a new operation. No shared case observes a conflict refusal. |
| RK-24.7-035 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | Shared listing and snapshot can inspect distinct paths with the same frontmatter name; the existing paging case repeats an identical URI. |
| RK-24.7-038 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The served registry session can exercise turn activation with declared prerequisites. Local prerequisite cases do not register a served skill. |
| RK-24.7-051 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The scripted registry can return a refused refreshed entry and report its reason. Existing mismatch cases refresh to an admissible entry, or report a generic verification failure. |
| RK-24.7-053 | §24.7 | phase6 | RMK-1939 | step: a realtime fixture skills option: no shared fixed-instructions realtime provider demonstrates the attributed body in an activation result. |
| RK-24.7-055 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | No shared activation compares the held entry with a later lookup or refuses a previously loaded skill that has since been marked unavailable. A blocked tool hook exercises a different condition. |
| RK-24.7-059 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The registry session supports a lookup error under a known label. No shared case observes the missing-skill diagnostic following that lookup. |
| RK-24.7-062 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The shared registry can inspect an active entry and resource requests. No case requests a file omitted from that entry; inactive-reference refusal is a different condition. |
| RK-24.7-066 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The scripted registry accepts multiple servers and records each request. No case asserts that a reference read on one produces no request on the other. |
| RK-24.7-068 | §24.7 | phase6 | RMK-1939 | step: a realtime fixture skills option: no shared realtime inline_full session asserts refusal and the reason for a served entry. |
| RK-24.7-070 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The served registry can list and read a nested metadata file. No case asserts it remains an ordinary file with no nested activation. |
| RK-24.7-071 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | Shared served skills can run through an installed text channel. No case asserts run_skill_script refuses execution of a served skill. |
| RK-24.7-077 | §24.7 | writable | RMK-1939 | The server fixture supports dynamic entries and captured approvals but no scenario asserts the missing fingerprint for one. |
| RK-24.7-079 | §24.7 | step | RMK-1939 | The built-in served cache is in memory, and the shared runner offers no deployment with a persistent disk cache in which to inspect these conditional properties. |
| RK-25.1-002 | §25.1 | step | RMK-1934 | Inspect the image and chat provider contracts on an implementation that provides both, using a provider descriptor or independent adapter fixture. Rust currently has no image provider. |
| RK-25.1-003 | §25.1 | step | RMK-1934 | Configure a conversational provider and a separately instrumented image provider with distinct vendor identifiers, credentials and instances, and observe independent calls. |
| RK-25.1-004 | §25.1 | step | RMK-1934 | Run a conversational agent whose tool or host invokes a different image provider and check which provider actually serves each request. |
| RK-25.1-005 | §25.1 | step | RMK-1934 | Inspect the declared public AIResponse.content type in each implementation and assert it is exclusively text. Native declarations specify str in Python and String in Rust. Existing response examples returning text cannot distinguish an additional allowed image type; the Python public API snapshot records parameter names without type annotations. No image generation call is necessary. |
| RK-25.2-002 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | Read the image provider's public name independently of the selected model. |
| RK-25.2-032 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | Select an image model and read the active model identifier from its provider. |
| RK-25.2-003 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | Read the provider's editing capability on configurations that do and do not accept references. |
| RK-25.2-004 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | List an image provider's model information without making a network request. |
| RK-25.2-005 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | Call the public generation operation with a prompt, size, requested count and references and inspect its image results. |
| RK-25.2-006 | §25.2 | step | RMK-1934 | Close a constructed image provider and observe release of its instrumented resources. |
| RK-25.3-002 | §25.3 | step | RMK-1934 | Read an ImageResult's encoded data and media type through a shared result or provider fixture. |
| RK-25.3-019 | §25.3 | step | RMK-1934 | Read the optional revised prompt from a constructed or returned ImageResult. |
| RK-25.3-020 | §25.3 | step | RMK-1934 | Read the image-generation usage map on a returned ImageResult. |
| RK-25.4-002 | §25.4 | step | RMK-1934 | Use the same generate entry point for prompt-only and reference-image calls and read the edited result. |
| RK-25.5-003 | §25.5 | step | RMK-1934 | Inspect the four integer text and image input and output counters on generation usage. Conversational usage fixtures provide preassembled counts. |
| RK-25.7-002 | §25.7 | step | RMK-1934 | Observe an ordinary inbound and broadcast pass while an enabled image provider records whether it was invoked as a stage. |
| RK-25.7-003 | §25.7 | step | RMK-1934 | Expose the implementation's complete public hook trigger vocabulary and compare it with the ordinary hooks. Scoped observed hook lists cannot establish the absence of a trigger. |
| RK-25.7-004 | §25.7 | step | RMK-1934 | Invoke the actual image provider from a tool handler or application and assert its recorded generation request. |
| RK-25.7-005 | §25.7 | step | RMK-1934 | Feed actual generated output through the public room integration path and observe inbound processing, delivery and visibility. Starting with a manually supplied image already on the ordinary route cannot detect a separate generation route. |

| RK-A.9-011 | §A.9 | step | RMK-1961 | No runner key sets tool_loop_timeout_seconds, and its 300-second default cannot be waited out under the suite's time rule. The audit's suspect on the configuration list is RMK-1962. |
| RK-A.9-042 | §A.9 | writable | RMK-1961 | A schema turn on a channel given skills, on a model that takes a schema but not beside tools, fails unsupported; the skills and model capability keys exist. |
| RK-A.9.1-003 | §A.9.1 | step | RMK-1961 | Needs a real stdio launch of an agent process, which the runners' scripted agents do not perform (as RK-6.4-486). |
| RK-A.9.1-004 | §A.9.1 | step | RMK-1961 | Needs a real agent launch from an argument vector, which the runners' scripted agents do not perform. |
| RK-A.9.1-010 | §A.9.1 | writable | RMK-1961 | Two messages to one room while the scripted agent holds the first prompt: the second is prompted only once the first returns. |
| RK-A.9.1-011 | §A.9.1 | step | RMK-1961 | No runner key sets an ACP agent's command: the runners' scripted agents take none. The text differs from Python's signature (RMK-1966). |

| RK-A.10-010 | §A.10 | writable | RMK-1961 | voice.stream with an after_tts hook: the hook fires once the stream completed (requires voice). |
